15:## 1. Codex Usage API
16:
17:**Sources:** OpenAI Codex source code (`github.com/openai/codex`, Rust codebase), CodexBar macOS app (`github.com/steipete/CodexBar`), Context7 Codex developer docs.
18:
19:### 1.1 Active Polling Endpoint
20:
21:```
22:GET https://chatgpt.com/backend-api/wham/usage
23:```
24:
25:Fallback (when base URL doesn't contain `/backend-api`):
26:```
27:GET {base_url}/api/codex/usage
28:```
29:
30:**Required Headers:**
31:```http
32:Authorization: Bearer <access_token>
33:ChatGPT-Account-Id: <account_id>
34:Content-Type: application/json
35:Accept: application/json
36:```
37:
38:- `access_token` — The OAuth access token from `auth.openai.com` (same token our `codex-oauth.ts` already obtains)
39:- `account_id` — Account UUID from OAuth token data. Stored in `~/.codex/auth.json` under `tokens.account_id`. Optional per CodexBar ("when available") but may be required.
40:
41:### 1.2 Response Schema
42:
43:From `codex-rs/codex-backend-openapi-models/src/models/rate_limit_status_payload.rs`:
44:
45:```json
46:{
47:  "plan_type": "plus",
48:  "rate_limit": {
49:    "allowed": true,
50:    "limit_reached": false,
51:    "primary_window": {
52:      "used_percent": 96,
53:      "limit_window_seconds": 18000,
54:      "reset_after_seconds": 673,
55:      "reset_at": 1730947200
56:    },
57:    "secondary_window": {
58:      "used_percent": 70,
59:      "limit_window_seconds": 604800,
60:      "reset_after_seconds": 43200,
61:      "reset_at": 1730980800
62:    }
63:  },
64:  "credits": {
65:    "has_credits": false,
66:    "unlimited": true,
67:    "balance": null
68:  },
69:  "additional_rate_limits": [
70:    {
71:      "limit_name": "codex_other",
72:      "metered_feature": "codex_other",
73:      "rate_limit": {
74:        "allowed": true,
75:        "limit_reached": false,
76:        "primary_window": {
77:          "used_percent": 70,
78:          "limit_window_seconds": 3600,
79:          "reset_after_seconds": 1800,
80:          "reset_at": 1730947200
81:        }
82:      }
83:    }
84:  ]
85:}
86:```
87:
88:- `primary_window` = 5h session (18000s). Maps to our `sessionPercent`.
89:- `secondary_window` = Weekly (604800s = 7d). Maps to our `weeklyPercent`.
90:- `reset_at` = Unix timestamp (seconds). Convert to ms for our `sessionResetTimestamp`/`weeklyResetTimestamp`.
91:- `plan_type` values: `guest`, `free`, `go`, `plus`, `pro`, `free_workspace`, `team`, `business`, `education`, `quorum`, `k12`, `enterprise`, `edu`
92:
93:### 1.3 Passive Headers (From API Responses)
94:
95:Rate limit data is also returned in HTTP response headers on every `/v1/responses` call:
96:
97:```
98:x-codex-primary-used-percent         → float (e.g., "25.0")
99:x-codex-primary-window-minutes       → integer (e.g., "300" for 5h)
100:x-codex-primary-reset-at             → unix timestamp seconds
101:x-codex-secondary-used-percent       → float (weekly)
102:x-codex-secondary-window-minutes     → integer
103:x-codex-secondary-reset-at           → unix timestamp seconds
104:x-codex-credits-has-credits          → "true" or "false"
105:x-codex-credits-unlimited            → "true" or "false"
106:x-codex-credits-balance              → decimal string e.g. "9.99"
107:```
108:
109:SSE event type `codex.rate_limits` also carries this data inline in streaming responses.
110:
111:### 1.4 Token Details
112:
113:Our `codex-oauth.ts` already uses the correct flow:
114:- **Client ID:** `app_EMoamEEZ73f0CkXaXp7hrann` (same as Codex CLI)
115:- **Auth endpoint:** `https://auth.openai.com/oauth/authorize`
116:- **Token endpoint:** `https://auth.openai.com/oauth/token`
117:- **Scopes:** `openid profile email offline_access`
118:- **Refresh:** `POST https://auth.openai.com/oauth/token` with `grant_type=refresh_token`
119:
120:**Missing:** `account_id` for the `ChatGPT-Account-Id` header. Options:
121:1. Decode from the JWT access token
122:2. Read from `~/.codex/auth.json` (`tokens.account_id`)
123:3. Extract during OAuth token exchange (may be in response)
124:4. Try without it first (optional per CodexBar docs)
125:

127:
128:## 2. Current System Architecture
129:
130:### 2.1 Two Parallel Account Systems
131:
132:The app has TWO account management systems that don't fully integrate:
133:
134:**System A: Legacy Claude Profile Manager (Main Process)**
135:- `claude-profile-manager.ts` — Manages OAuth profiles, rate limits, usage, auto-swap
136:- `claude-profiles.json` — Stores profiles with `activeProfileId`, `accountPriorityOrder`
137:- `usage-monitor.ts` — Polls Anthropic's `/api/oauth/usage` endpoint every 30s
138:- `token-refresh.ts` — Refreshes tokens via `console.anthropic.com/v1/oauth/token`
139:- `rate-limit-detector.ts` — Detects rate limits, triggers auto-swap
140:- `profile-scorer.ts` — Scores profiles by availability for auto-swap
141:- **100% Anthropic-specific.** Only knows about Anthropic OAuth tokens, Anthropic endpoints, Anthropic keychain format.
142:
143:**System B: Multi-Provider Accounts (Renderer + Settings)**
144:- `ProviderAccount[]` in `settings-store.ts` — All connected accounts (any provider)
145:- `globalPriorityOrder: string[]` in AppSettings — Manual priority queue
146:- `useActiveProvider()` hook — First account in priority order = active
147:- **Provider-agnostic.** Works for all 10 providers. But has NO usage monitoring, NO auto-swap.
148:
149:**The gap:** System A handles usage monitoring + auto-swap but only for Anthropic. System B handles multi-provider accounts but has no usage awareness.
150:
151:### 2.2 Data Flow: Usage Polling
152:
153:```
154:UsageMonitor.start() → 30s interval
155:  ↓
156:checkUsageAndSwap()
157:  ├─ determineActiveProfile()           ← Hardcoded: defaults to anthropic baseUrl
158:  ├─ getCredential()                    ← Hardcoded: reads from Anthropic keychain
159:  │   └─ ensureValidToken(configDir)    ← Hardcoded: refreshes via Anthropic endpoint
160:  ├─ fetchUsageViaAPI()                 ← Hardcoded: only allows anthropic/zai/zhipu domains
161:  │   ├─ getUsageEndpoint(provider)     ← Only 3 providers configured
162:  │   ├─ Add anthropic-specific headers ← if (provider === 'anthropic') add beta headers
163:  │   └─ Parse response                ← Provider-specific normalization
164:  ├─ emit('usage-updated')             → IPC 'claude:usageUpdated' → renderer
165:  ├─ emit('all-profiles-usage-updated') → IPC 'claude:allProfilesUsageUpdated' → renderer
166:  └─ checkThresholdsExceeded()
167:     └─ performProactiveSwap()          ← Only swaps Anthropic profiles
168:```
169:
170:### 2.3 Data Flow: Account Swapping
171:
172:**Manual swap (UI):**
173:```
174:User clicks account in UsageIndicator popover
175:  → handleSwapAccount(accountId)
176:  → setQueueOrder([accountId, ...rest])    ← Reorders globalPriorityOrder
177:  → requestUsageUpdate()                   ← Refreshes usage display
178:```
179:
180:**Automatic swap (rate limit hit):**
181:```
182:SDK operation fails with 429
183:  → detectRateLimit(output)                ← Pattern: "Limit reached · resets..."
184:  → recordRateLimitEvent(profileId)
185:  → getBestAvailableProfileEnv()
186:  → profileManager.setActiveProfile()      ← Only updates claude-profiles.json
187:  → usageMonitor.getAllProfilesUsage()     ← Refreshes UI
188:  ← Returns new profile env vars
189:```
190:
191:**Problem:** Auto-swap updates `claude-profiles.json` but NOT `globalPriorityOrder`. The renderer's priority queue may be out of sync.
192:
193:### 2.4 UI Components
194:
195:| Component | What it shows | Provider-specific? |
196:|---|---|---|
197:| `AuthStatusIndicator` | Provider badge (OpenAI/Anthropic) + auth type label | Codex = green "Codex", Anthropic = orange "OAuth" |
198:| `UsageIndicator` | Usage bars OR "Subscription" OR "Unlimited" | Anthropic OAuth = bars, Codex OAuth = "Subscription", API = "Unlimited" |
199:| `ProviderAccountCard` | Account card in settings with usage bars | Shows usage bars only when `account.usage` populated (Anthropic only) |
200:| `ProviderAccountsList` | All accounts grouped by provider | Generic, but re-auth routes differ per provider |
201:| `AddAccountDialog` | OAuth flow + account creation | Different flows: Codex → `codexAuthLogin()`, Anthropic → `claudeAuthLoginSubprocess()` |
202:| `ProviderSection` | Provider group with "Add" buttons | Button label: "Add Codex Subscription" vs "Add OAuth" |
203:
204:### 2.5 Type Naming
205:
206:Types use "Claude" prefix but are structurally generic:
207:```typescript
208:ClaudeUsageSnapshot    → { sessionPercent, weeklyPercent, resetTimestamps, profileId, ... }
209:ClaudeUsageData        → { sessionUsagePercent, weeklyUsagePercent }
210:ClaudeRateLimitEvent   → { type, hitAt, resetAt }
211:ProfileUsageSummary    → { sessionPercent, weeklyPercent, availabilityScore, ... }
212:AllProfilesUsage       → { activeProfile, allProfiles[], fetchedAt }
213:```
214:
215:These types work perfectly for Codex data — same session/weekly model. No structural changes needed, just need to populate them.

271:
272:## 5. Implementation Plan
273:
274:### Phase 1: Codex Usage Fetcher (Core)
275:
276:Create `apps/desktop/src/main/claude-profile/codex-usage-fetcher.ts`:
277:
278:```typescript
279:// Responsibilities:
280:// 1. Read Codex OAuth token (from our codex-auth.json)
281:// 2. Read account_id (from ~/.codex/auth.json or JWT decode)
282:// 3. Call GET https://chatgpt.com/backend-api/wham/usage
283:// 4. Parse response into ClaudeUsageSnapshot format
284:// 5. Handle 401 → refresh token via codex-oauth.ts
285:// 6. Handle 403 → mark as needsReauthentication
286:```
287:
288:**Key function:**
289:```typescript
290:async function fetchCodexUsage(accessToken: string, accountId?: string): Promise<ClaudeUsageSnapshot>
291:```
292:
293:### Phase 2: Wire into Usage Monitor
294:
295:Modify `usage-monitor.ts`:
296:
297:1. Add `chatgpt.com` to `ALLOWED_USAGE_API_DOMAINS`
298:2. Add Codex to `PROVIDER_USAGE_ENDPOINTS`
299:3. Update `determineActiveProfile()` to detect Codex accounts from `globalPriorityOrder`
300:4. Update `getCredential()` to read Codex OAuth token (from `codex-auth.json`)
301:5. Update `fetchUsageViaAPI()` to handle Codex response format
302:6. Add Codex-specific headers (`ChatGPT-Account-Id`)
303:7. Add Codex response parsing (different JSON structure than Anthropic)
304:
305:### Phase 3: Token Refresh Routing
306:
307:Modify `token-refresh.ts` or create parallel Codex path:
308:
309:- When refreshing a Codex token, use `auth.openai.com/oauth/token` with Codex client ID
310:- When refreshing an Anthropic token, use `console.anthropic.com/v1/oauth/token` with Claude client ID
311:- Provider detection: check the account's `provider` field, or detect from token prefix
312:
313:### Phase 4: UI Updates
314:
315:1. `UsageIndicator.tsx:118` — Add `|| provider === 'openai'` to `hasUsageMonitoring`
316:2. That's it — the rest of the UI already handles usage bars, reset times, multi-profile display generically
317:
318:### Phase 5: Auto-Swap for Codex
319:
320:1. Add Codex-specific rate limit patterns to `rate-limit-detector.ts`
321:2. Codex returns `"codexErrorInfo": "UsageLimitExceeded"` on limit hit
322:3. Auto-swap logic in `profile-scorer.ts` already works — it just needs usage data populated
323:

325:
326:## Appendix: Comparison Table
327:
328:| Aspect | Anthropic (Claude Code) | OpenAI (Codex) |
329:|---|---|---|
330:| **Usage endpoint** | `api.anthropic.com/api/oauth/usage` | `chatgpt.com/backend-api/wham/usage` |
331:| **Auth header** | `Bearer <oauth_token>` | `Bearer <access_token>` + `ChatGPT-Account-Id` |
332:| **Session window** | ~5h | Configurable (`limit_window_seconds`) |
333:| **Weekly window** | 7 days | Configurable (`limit_window_seconds`) |
334:| **Token source** | Keychain (`Claude Code-credentials`) | File (`codex-auth.json`) |
335:| **Token refresh** | `console.anthropic.com/v1/oauth/token` | `auth.openai.com/oauth/token` |
336:| **Client ID** | `9d1c250a-e61b-44d9-88ed-5944d1962f5e` | `app_EMoamEEZ73f0CkXaXp7hrann` |
337:| **Passive tracking** | Not available | `x-codex-*` response headers |
338:| **Rate limit error** | `"Limit reached · resets Dec 17..."` | `"codexErrorInfo": "UsageLimitExceeded"` |
339:| **Profile isolation** | `~/.claude-profiles/{name}/` dirs | Single `codex-auth.json` file |
340:| **Multi-account** | Multiple config dirs in keychain | Single file (no multi-account yet) |
341:
342:## Appendix: Caveats
343:
344:1. **Undocumented API** — `chatgpt.com/backend-api/wham/usage` is internal. The Codex CLI depends on it, so it's unlikely to break silently.
345:2. **Account ID** — May be required. Test without it first. If needed, decode from JWT or read `~/.codex/auth.json`.
346:3. **CORS** — Not an issue (Electron main process = Node.js).
347:4. **Polling rate** — Unknown if OpenAI rate-limits `wham/usage`. Start conservatively (every 30-60s).
348:5. **Multi-account Codex** — Codex CLI doesn't support multiple accounts. We store one token file. If user has multiple Codex accounts, they'd need to re-auth each time (unlike Anthropic which supports multiple config dirs).
[31;1mInvalidOperation: [31;1mCannot set property. Property setting is supported only on core types in this language mode.[0m

 succeeded in 398ms:
1:# cc-cache-audit
2:
3:Claude Code v2.1.69+ injects a dynamic billing header into the system prompt that breaks cross-session prompt cache sharing. This repo contains the A/B test that proves it and the one-line fix.
4:
5:## The Fix
6:
7:```bash
8:echo 'export CLAUDE_CODE_ATTRIBUTION_HEADER=false' >> ~/.zshrc
9:source ~/.zshrc
10:```
11:
12:Only affects new sessions. Existing ones keep running fine.
13:
14:## Root Cause
15:
16:Since v2.1.69, Claude Code prepends an `x-anthropic-billing-header` string to the system prompt:
17:
18:```
19:x-anthropic-billing-header: cc_version=2.1.88.a3f; cc_entrypoint=cli; cch=00000;
20:```
21:
22:That `.a3f` suffix is a 3-char SHA-256 hash computed from the first user message in each conversation:
23:
24:```javascript
25:// Deobfuscated from cli.js
26:function computeHash(firstUserMessage, version) {
27:  const chars = [4, 7, 20].map(i => firstUserMessage[i] || "0").join("");
28:  return sha256("59cf53e54c78" + chars + version).slice(0, 3);
29:}
30:```
31:
32:Different conversation → different first message → different hash → different system prompt prefix.
33:
34:This also affects **subagents within the same conversation**. Each Agent tool call has its own message context, so its first "user message" (the agent prompt) produces a different hash. Verified in a real session — 3 distinct hashes in one conversation:
35:
36:```
37:Main conversation:  cc_version=2.1.88.a3f  (34 API calls)
38:Subagent 1:         cc_version=2.1.88.e91  (3 API calls)
39:Subagent 2:         cc_version=2.1.88.7c2  (3 API calls)
40:```
41:
42:Anthropic's prompt cache uses [prefix matching](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/prompt-caching) with no per-session isolation — cache is shared at the Organization/Workspace level. So all Claude Code sessions and subagents *should* share the same cached system prompt. But the billing header makes each one's prefix unique, causing the system prompt (~12K tokens) to get rebuilt from scratch every time.
43:
44:## A/B Test
45:
46:4 sessions per round, each with a different prompt, sequential with 8-second gaps (within the 5-min cache TTL). All sessions use `claude --print` mode.
47:
48:### Round A — Header ON (default)
49:
50:| Session | Prompt | cache_read | cache_creation | hit ratio |
51:|---------|--------|-----------|----------------|-----------|
52:| 1 | What is the capital of France? | 11,272 | 12,206 | 48.0% |
53:| 2 | List three prime numbers under 20. | 11,272 | 12,202 | 48.0% |
54:| 3 | Explain what a goroutine is in one sentence. | 11,374 | 12,753 | 47.1% |
55:| 4 | Name one benefit of TypeScript over JavaScript. | 11,272 | 12,203 | 48.0% |
56:
57:Every session rebuilds ~12K tokens of cache.
58:
59:### Round B — Header OFF
60:
61:| Session | Prompt | cache_read | cache_creation | hit ratio |
62:|---------|--------|-----------|----------------|-----------|
63:| 1 | What is the capital of France? | 23,478 | 0 | 99.98% |
64:| 2 | List three prime numbers under 20. | 23,474 | 0 | 99.98% |
65:| 3 | Explain what a goroutine is in one sentence. | 11,272 | 12,197 | 48.0% |
66:| 4 | Name one benefit of TypeScript over JavaScript. | 23,475 | 0 | 99.98% |
67:
68:3 out of 4 sessions: zero cache creation, full cache read. Session 3 was a server-side cache eviction — cache came right back for session 4.
69:
70:### What the Numbers Mean
71:
72:The data shows a two-tier caching pattern, consistent with the Anthropic API's cache hierarchy (`tools → system → messages`):
73:
74:- **Tools block (~11,272 tokens)**: Tool definitions are stable across sessions — always cached regardless of header setting.
75:- **System prompt block (~12,200 tokens)**: Contains the billing header with a per-session hash — rebuilt every session when header is ON.
76:
77:With the header OFF, both blocks are fully cacheable → 99.98% hit ratio.
78:
79:### Cost Impact
80:
81:Using Anthropic pricing (cache_read = 0.1x base, cache_creation = 1.25x base):
82:
83:| | Header ON (per session) | Header OFF (per session) |
84:|--|----------------------|------------------------|
85:| Effective cost | 11,272×0.1 + 12,200×1.25 + 3 ≈ **16,380 equiv tokens** | 23,475×0.1 + 0 + 3 ≈ **2,351 equiv tokens** |
86:| | baseline | **~85% reduction (~7x cheaper)** |
87:
88:This measures system prompt cost only. For long conversations where user messages dominate, the relative savings decrease.
89:
90:## FAQ
91:
92:**Will this get me banned?**
93:
94:No reports of anyone being banned or warned. [claude-code-router](https://github.com/musistudio/claude-code-router/pull/1220) and [CLIProxyAPI](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/issues/1592) ship with this disabled in production. The env var is a proper feature toggle in the source code.
95:
96:**Why did Anthropic add this?**
97:
98:Internal billing attribution — tracking which Claude Code version and entrypoint (CLI, SDK, GitHub Action) made each API call. It's in the system prompt instead of HTTP headers probably because Bedrock/Vertex don't forward custom headers. The API has a `metadata` field designed for exactly this kind of thing.
99:
100:**Do I need to restart existing sessions?**
101:
102:No. Old sessions use their own cache keys and don't interfere with new ones.
103:
104:## Caveats
105:
106:- Tested with `claude --print` mode only. Interactive mode uses a larger system prompt — absolute numbers will differ, but the mechanism is the same.
107:- Sample size is n=4 per round. The pattern is clear but more runs would strengthen confidence.
108:- Server-side cache eviction is non-deterministic (see session B-3).
109:
110:## Run It Yourself
111:
112:```bash
113:chmod +x run-test.sh
114:./run-test.sh
115:```
116:
117:Runs 4 sessions with the header ON, then 4 with it OFF. Extracts `cache_read_input_tokens` from session JSONL files and generates a raw comparison in `results/`.
118:
119:The detailed report with full analysis is in [`report.md`](report.md).
120:
121:Requires `claude` CLI installed and authenticated. Note: the script outputs local JSONL paths in metrics files — scrub them before publishing.

59:

64:```

66:### Quick Install (Linux/macOS)
67:
68:```bash
69:curl -fsSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/rtk-ai/rtk/refs/heads/master/install.sh | sh
70:```
71:
72:> Installs to `~/.local/bin`. Add to PATH if needed:
73:> ```bash
74:> echo 'export PATH="$HOME/.local/bin:$PATH"' >> ~/.bashrc  # or ~/.zshrc
75:> ```
76:
77:### Cargo
78:
79:```bash
80:cargo install --git https://github.com/rtk-ai/rtk
81:```
82:
83:### Pre-built Binaries
84:
85:Download from [releases](https://github.com/rtk-ai/rtk/releases):
86:- macOS: `rtk-x86_64-apple-darwin.tar.gz` / `rtk-aarch64-apple-darwin.tar.gz`
87:- Linux: `rtk-x86_64-unknown-linux-musl.tar.gz` / `rtk-aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu.tar.gz`
88:- Windows: `rtk-x86_64-pc-windows-msvc.zip`
89:
90:> **Windows users**: Extract the zip and place `rtk.exe` somewhere in your PATH (e.g. `C:\Users\<you>\.local\bin`). Run RTK from **Command Prompt**, **PowerShell**, or **Windows Terminal** — do not double-click the `.exe` (it will flash and close). For the best experience, use [WSL](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/wsl/install) where the full hook system works natively. See [Windows setup](#windows) below for details.

93:

95:rtk --version   # Should show "rtk 0.28.2"
96:rtk gain        # Should show token savings stats
97:```

100:
101:## Quick Start
102:
103:```bash
104:# 1. Install for your AI tool
105:rtk init -g                     # Claude Code / Copilot (default)
106:rtk init -g --gemini            # Gemini CLI
107:rtk init -g --codex             # Codex (OpenAI)
108:rtk init -g --agent cursor      # Cursor
109:rtk init --agent windsurf       # Windsurf
110:rtk init --agent cline          # Cline / Roo Code
111:rtk init --agent kilocode       # Kilo Code
112:rtk init --agent antigravity    # Google Antigravity
113:
114:# 2. Restart your AI tool, then test
115:git status  # Automatically rewritten to rtk git status
116:```
117:
118:The hook transparently rewrites Bash commands (e.g., `git status` -> `rtk git status`) before execution. Claude never sees the rewrite, it just gets compressed output.
119:
120:**Important:** the hook only runs on Bash tool calls. Claude Code built-in tools like `Read`, `Grep`, and `Glob` do not pass through the Bash hook, so they are not auto-rewritten. To get RTK's compact output for those workflows, use shell commands (`cat`/`head`/`tail`, `rg`/`grep`, `find`) or call `rtk read`, `rtk grep`, or `rtk find` directly.
121:
122:## How It Works
123:
124:```
125:  Without rtk:                                    With rtk:
126:
127:  Claude  --git status-->  shell  -->  git         Claude  --git status-->  RTK  -->  git
128:    ^                                   |            ^                      |          |
129:    |        ~2,000 tokens (raw)        |            |   ~200 tokens        | filter   |
130:    +-----------------------------------+            +------- (filtered) ---+----------+
131:```
132:
133:Four strategies applied per command type:
134:
135:1. **Smart Filtering** - Removes noise (comments, whitespace, boilerplate)
136:2. **Grouping** - Aggregates similar items (files by directory, errors by type)
137:3. **Truncation** - Keeps relevant context, cuts redundancy
138:4. **Deduplication** - Collapses repeated log lines with counts
139:

293:
294:## Auto-Rewrite Hook
295:
296:The most effective way to use rtk. The hook transparently intercepts Bash commands and rewrites them to rtk equivalents before execution.
297:
298:**Result**: 100% rtk adoption across all conversations and subagents, zero token overhead.
299:
300:**Scope note:** this only applies to Bash tool calls. Claude Code built-in tools such as `Read`, `Grep`, and `Glob` bypass the hook, so use shell commands or explicit `rtk` commands when you want RTK filtering there.
301:
302:### Setup
303:
304:```bash
305:rtk init -g                 # Install hook + RTK.md (recommended)
306:rtk init -g --opencode      # OpenCode plugin (instead of Claude Code)
307:rtk init -g --auto-patch    # Non-interactive (CI/CD)
308:rtk init -g --hook-only     # Hook only, no RTK.md
309:rtk init --show             # Verify installation
310:```
311:
312:After install, **restart Claude Code**.
313:
314:## Windows
315:
316:RTK works on Windows with some limitations. The auto-rewrite hook (`rtk-rewrite.sh`) requires a Unix shell, so on native Windows RTK falls back to **CLAUDE.md injection mode** — your AI assistant receives RTK instructions but commands are not rewritten automatically.
317:
318:### Recommended: WSL (full support)
319:
320:For the best experience, use [WSL](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/wsl/install) (Windows Subsystem for Linux). Inside WSL, RTK works exactly like Linux — full hook support, auto-rewrite, everything:
321:
322:```bash
323:# Inside WSL
324:curl -fsSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/rtk-ai/rtk/refs/heads/master/install.sh | sh
325:rtk init -g
326:```
327:
328:### Native Windows (limited support)
329:
330:On native Windows (cmd.exe / PowerShell), RTK filters work but the hook does not auto-rewrite commands:
331:
332:```powershell
333:# 1. Download and extract rtk-x86_64-pc-windows-msvc.zip from releases
334:# 2. Add rtk.exe to your PATH
335:# 3. Initialize (falls back to CLAUDE.md injection)
336:rtk init -g
337:# 4. Use rtk explicitly
338:rtk cargo test
339:rtk git status
340:```
341:
342:**Important**: Do not double-click `rtk.exe` — it is a CLI tool that prints usage and exits immediately. Always run it from a terminal (Command Prompt, PowerShell, or Windows Terminal).
343:
344:| Feature | WSL | Native Windows |
345:|---------|-----|----------------|
346:| Filters (cargo, git, etc.) | Full | Full |
347:| Auto-rewrite hook | Yes | No (CLAUDE.md fallback) |
348:| `rtk init -g` | Hook mode | CLAUDE.md mode |
349:| `rtk gain` / analytics | Full | Full |
350:
351:## Supported AI Tools
352:
353:RTK supports 12 AI coding tools. Each integration transparently rewrites shell commands to `rtk` equivalents for 60-90% token savings.
354:
355:| Tool | Install | Method |
356:|------|---------|--------|
357:| **Claude Code** | `rtk init -g` | PreToolUse hook (bash) |
358:| **GitHub Copilot (VS Code)** | `rtk init -g --copilot` | PreToolUse hook — transparent rewrite |
359:| **GitHub Copilot CLI** | `rtk init -g --copilot` | PreToolUse deny-with-suggestion (CLI limitation) |
360:| **Cursor** | `rtk init -g --agent cursor` | preToolUse hook (hooks.json) |
361:| **Gemini CLI** | `rtk init -g --gemini` | BeforeTool hook |
362:| **Codex** | `rtk init -g --codex` | AGENTS.md + RTK.md instructions |
363:| **Windsurf** | `rtk init --agent windsurf` | .windsurfrules (project-scoped) |
364:| **Cline / Roo Code** | `rtk init --agent cline` | .clinerules (project-scoped) |
365:| **OpenCode** | `rtk init -g --opencode` | Plugin TS (tool.execute.before) |
366:| **OpenClaw** | `openclaw plugins install ./openclaw` | Plugin TS (before_tool_call) |
367:| **Mistral Vibe** | Planned ([#800](https://github.com/rtk-ai/rtk/issues/800)) | Blocked on upstream |
368:| **Kilo Code** | `rtk init --agent kilocode` | .kilocode/rules/rtk-rules.md (project-scoped) |
369:| **Google Antigravity** | `rtk init --agent antigravity` | .agents/rules/antigravity-rtk-rules.md (project-scoped) |
370:
371:For per-agent setup details, override controls, and graceful degradation, see the [Supported Agents guide](https://www.rtk-ai.app/guide/getting-started/supported-agents).
372:
373:## Configuration
374:
375:`~/.config/rtk/config.toml` (macOS: `~/Library/Application Support/rtk/config.toml`):
376:
377:```toml
378:[hooks]
379:exclude_commands = ["curl", "playwright"]  # skip rewrite for these
380:
381:[tee]
382:enabled = true          # save raw output on failure (default: true)
383:mode = "failures"       # "failures", "always", or "never"
384:```
385:
386:When a command fails, RTK saves the full unfiltered output so the LLM can read it without re-executing:
387:
388:```
389:FAILED: 2/15 tests
390:[full output: ~/.local/share/rtk/tee/1707753600_cargo_test.log]
391:```
392:
393:For the full config reference (all sections, env vars, per-project filters), see the [Configuration guide](https://www.rtk-ai.app/guide/getting-started/configuration).
394:

399:cargo uninstall rtk          # Remove binary
400:brew uninstall rtk           # If installed via Homebrew
401:```

411:## Privacy & Telemetry
412:
413:RTK can collect **anonymous, aggregate usage metrics** once per day. Telemetry is **disabled by default** and requires **explicit opt-in consent** (GDPR Art. 6, 7) during `rtk init` or via `rtk telemetry enable`. This data helps us build a better product: identifying which commands need filters, which filters need improvement, and how much value RTK delivers. For the full list of fields, data handling, and contributor guidelines, see **[docs/TELEMETRY.md](docs/TELEMETRY.md)**.
414:
415:**What is collected and why:**
416:
417:| Category | Data | Why |
418:|----------|------|-----|
419:| Identity | Salted device hash (SHA-256, not reversible) | Count unique installations without tracking individuals |
420:| Environment | RTK version, OS, architecture, install method | Know which platforms to support and test |
421:| Usage volume | Command count (24h), total commands, tokens saved (24h/30d/total) | Measure adoption and value delivered |
422:| Quality | Top 5 passthrough commands (0% savings), parse failure count, commands with <30% savings | Identify missing filters and weak ones to improve |
423:| Ecosystem | Command category distribution (e.g. git 45%, cargo 20%, js 15%) | Prioritize filter development for popular ecosystems |
424:| Retention | Days since first use, active days in last 30 | Understand engagement and detect churn |
425:| Adoption | AI agent hook type (claude/gemini/codex), custom TOML filter count | Track integration coverage and DSL adoption |
426:| Configuration | Whether config.toml exists, number of excluded commands, project count | Understand user maturity and customization patterns |
427:| Features | Usage counts for meta-commands (gain, discover, proxy, verify) | Know which RTK features are valued vs unused |
428:| Economics | Estimated USD savings (based on API token pricing) | Quantify the value RTK provides to users |
429:
430:All data is **aggregate counts or anonymized command names** (first 3 words, no arguments). Top commands report only tool names (e.g. "git", "cargo"), never full command lines.
431:
432:**What is NOT collected:** source code, file paths, command arguments, secrets, environment variables, personal data, or repository contents.
433:
434:**Manage telemetry:**
435:```bash
436:rtk telemetry status     # Check current consent state
437:rtk telemetry enable     # Give consent (interactive prompt)
438:rtk telemetry disable    # Withdraw consent — stops all collection immediately
439:rtk telemetry forget     # Withdraw consent + delete all local data + request server-side erasure
440:```
441:
442:**Override via environment:**
443:```bash
444:export RTK_TELEMETRY_DISABLED=1   # Blocks telemetry regardless of consent
445:```

31:# Claw Compactor
32:
33:### 14-Stage Fusion Pipeline for LLM Token Compression
34:
35:![Claw Compactor Banner](assets/banner.png)
36:
37:[![CI](https://github.com/open-compress/claw-compactor/actions/workflows/ci.yml/badge.svg)](https://github.com/open-compress/claw-compactor/actions)
38:[![codecov](https://codecov.io/gh/open-compress/claw-compactor/graph/badge.svg)](https://codecov.io/gh/open-compress/claw-compactor)
39:[![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/python-3.9%2B-blue)](https://python.org)
40:[![License](https://img.shields.io/badge/license-MIT-purple)](LICENSE)
41:[![PyPI](https://img.shields.io/pypi/v/claw-compactor?color=blue&label=PyPI)](https://pypi.org/project/claw-compactor/)
42:[![Downloads](https://img.shields.io/pypi/dm/claw-compactor?color=green&label=downloads)](https://pypi.org/project/claw-compactor/)
43:[![Stars](https://img.shields.io/github/stars/open-compress/claw-compactor?style=social)](https://github.com/open-compress/claw-compactor)
44:
45:**15–82% compression depending on content &middot; Zero LLM inference cost &middot; Reversible &middot; 1600+ tests**
46:
47:[Documentation](https://open-compress.github.io/claw-compactor) &middot; [Architecture](ARCHITECTURE.md) &middot; [Benchmarks](#benchmarks) &middot; [Quick Start](#quick-start) &middot; [API](#api)

52:
53:## What is Claw Compactor?
54:
55:Claw Compactor is an open-source **LLM token compression engine** built around a 14-stage **Fusion Pipeline**. Each stage is a specialized compressor — from AST-aware code analysis to JSON statistical sampling to simhash-based deduplication — chained through an immutable data flow architecture where each stage's output feeds the next.
56:
57:### Demo
58:
59:```
60:$ claw-compactor benchmark ./my-workspace
61:
62:  Claw Compactor v7.0 — Fusion Pipeline Benchmark
63:  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────
64:
65:  Scanning workspace... 47 files, 234,891 tokens
66:
67:  Stage Results:
68:  ┌──────────────────┬──────────┬───────────┬──────────┐
69:  │ Stage            │ Applied  │ Reduction │ Time     │
70:  ├──────────────────┼──────────┼───────────┼──────────┤
71:  │ Cortex           │ 47/47    │ —         │ 12ms     │
72:  │ Photon           │ 3/47     │ 2.1%      │ 4ms      │
73:  │ RLE              │ 41/47    │ 8.3%      │ 6ms      │
74:  │ SemanticDedup    │ 47/47    │ 12.7%     │ 18ms     │
75:  │ Ionizer          │ 8/47     │ 71.2%     │ 9ms      │
76:  │ Neurosyntax      │ 23/47    │ 18.4%     │ 31ms     │
77:  │ TokenOpt         │ 47/47    │ 4.1%      │ 3ms      │
78:  │ Abbrev           │ 12/47    │ 6.8%      │ 5ms      │
79:  └──────────────────┴──────────┴───────────┴──────────┘
80:
81:  Summary:
82:    Before:  234,891 tokens ($2.35 at GPT-4 rates)
83:    After:   108,250 tokens ($1.08)
84:    Saved:   126,641 tokens (53.9%) — $1.27/run
85:    Time:    88ms total
86:
87:  Estimated monthly savings at 100 runs/day: $3,810
88:```
89:
90:---
91:
92:## How It Compares
93:
94:| Feature | Claw Compactor | LLMLingua-2 | SelectiveContext | gzip + base64 |
95:|:--------|:-:|:-:|:-:|:-:|
96:| Compression rate | 15–82% | 30–70% | 10–40% | 60–80% |
97:| ROUGE-L @ 0.3 | **0.653** | 0.346 | ~0.4 | N/A |
98:| ROUGE-L @ 0.5 | **0.723** | 0.570 | ~0.6 | N/A |
99:| LLM inference cost | **$0** | ~$0.02/call | **$0** | **$0** |
100:| Latency | **<50ms** | ~300ms | ~200ms | <10ms |
101:| Reversible | **Yes** | No | No | Yes (manual) |
102:| Content-aware routing | **14 stages** | 1 (perplexity) | 1 (self-info) | None |
103:| AST-aware code handling | **Yes** (tree-sitter) | No | No | No |
104:| JSON schema sampling | **Yes** | No | No | No |
105:| Log/diff/search stages | **Yes** | No | No | No |
106:| Required dependencies | **0** | torch, transformers | torch | zlib |
107:| LLM-readable output | **Yes** | Partial | Partial | **No** |
108:
109:**Why Claw Compactor wins:** LLMLingua-2 drops tokens by perplexity score — effective for natural language, but destroys code identifiers, JSON keys, and log patterns. Claw Compactor uses content-type-aware stages that understand the structure of what they're compressing.
110:
111:---
112:
113:```
114:Input
115:  |
116:  v
117:┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
118:│                         FUSION PIPELINE                                 │
119:│                                                                         │
120:│  QuantumLock ─> Cortex ─> Photon ─> RLE ─> SemanticDedup ─> Ionizer    │
121:│       |            |         |        |          |              |        │
122:│   KV-cache    auto-detect  base64   path     simhash       JSON         │
123:│   alignment   16 languages  strip  shorten   dedup        sampling      │
124:│                                                                         │
125:│  ─> LogCrunch ─> SearchCrunch ─> DiffCrunch ─> StructuralCollapse      │
126:│        |              |              |                |                  │
127:│    log folding    result dedup   context fold    import merge            │
128:│                                                                         │
129:│  ─> Neurosyntax ─> Nexus ─> TokenOpt ─> Abbrev ─────────> Output       │
130:│        |             |          |           |                            │
131:│    AST compress   ML token   format     NL shorten                      │
132:│    (tree-sitter)  classify   optimize   (text only)                     │
133:│                                                                         │
134:│  [ RewindStore ] ── hash-addressed LRU for reversible retrieval         │
135:└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
136:```
137:
138:Key design principles:
139:
140:- **Immutable data flow** — `FusionContext` is a frozen dataclass. Every stage produces a new `FusionResult`; nothing is mutated in-place.
141:- **Gate-before-compress** — Each stage has `should_apply()` that inspects context type, language, and role before doing any work. Stages that don't apply are skipped at zero cost.
142:- **Content-aware routing** — Cortex auto-detects content type (code, JSON, logs, diffs, search results) and language (Python, Go, Rust, TypeScript, etc.), then downstream stages make type-aware compression decisions.
143:- **Reversible compression** — Ionizer stores originals in a hash-addressed `RewindStore`. The LLM can call a tool to retrieve any compressed section by its marker ID.
144:
145:---
146:
147:## Benchmarks
148:
149:### Real-World Compression (FusionEngine v7 vs Legacy Regex)
150:
151:| Content Type | Legacy | FusionEngine | Improvement |
152:|:-------------|-------:|-------------:|:-----------:|
153:| Python source | 7.3% | **25.0%** | 3.4x |
154:| JSON (100 items) | 12.6% | **81.9%** | 6.5x |
155:| Build logs | 5.5% | **24.1%** | 4.4x |
156:| Agent conversation | 5.7% | **31.0%** | 5.4x |
157:| Git diff | 6.2% | **15.0%** | 2.4x |
158:| Search results | 5.3% | **40.7%** | 7.7x |
159:| **Weighted average** | **9.2%** | **36.3%** | **3.9x** |
160:
161:### SWE-bench Real Tasks
162:
163:Tested on real SWE-bench instances with actual repository code:
164:
165:| Instance | Size | Compression |
166:|:---------|-----:|------------:|
167:| django__django-11620 | 4.5K | **14.5%** |
168:| sympy__sympy-14396 | 5.5K | **19.1%** |
169:| scikit-learn-25747 | 11.8K | **15.9%** |
170:| scikit-learn-13554 | 73K | **11.8%** |
171:| scikit-learn-25308 | 81K | **14.4%** |
172:
173:### vs LLMLingua-2 (ROUGE-L Fidelity)
174:
175:| Compression Rate | Claw Compactor | LLMLingua-2 | Delta |
176:|:-----------------|---------------:|------------:|------:|
177:| 0.3 (aggressive) | **0.653** | 0.346 | +88.2% |
178:| 0.5 (balanced) | **0.723** | 0.570 | +26.8% |
179:
180:Claw Compactor preserves more semantic content at the same compression ratio, with zero LLM inference cost.

184:## Quick Start
185:
186:### Install from PyPI
187:
188:```bash
189:pip install claw-compactor
190:```
191:
192:### Or clone from source
193:
194:```bash
195:git clone https://github.com/open-compress/claw-compactor.git
196:cd claw-compactor
197:pip install -e .
198:```
199:
200:### Run
201:
202:```bash
203:# Benchmark your workspace (non-destructive)
204:claw-compactor benchmark /path/to/workspace
205:
206:# Full compression pipeline
207:claw-compactor compress /path/to/workspace
208:```
209:
210:**Requirements:** Python 3.9+. Optional: `pip install claw-compactor[accurate]` for exact token counts via tiktoken.

212:---
213:
214:## API
215:
216:### FusionEngine — Single Text
217:
218:```python
219:from scripts.lib.fusion.engine import FusionEngine
220:
221:engine = FusionEngine()
222:
223:result = engine.compress(
224:    text="def hello():\n    # greeting function\n    print('hello')",
225:    content_type="code",    # or let Cortex auto-detect
226:    language="python",      # optional hint
227:)
228:
229:print(result["compressed"])     # compressed output
230:print(result["stats"])          # per-stage timing + token counts
231:print(result["markers"])        # Rewind markers for reversibility
232:```
233:
234:### FusionEngine — Chat Messages
235:
236:```python
237:messages = [
238:    {"role": "system", "content": "You are a coding assistant..."},
239:    {"role": "user", "content": "Fix the auth bug in login.py"},
240:    {"role": "assistant", "content": "I found the issue. Here's the fix:\n```python\n..."},
241:    {"role": "tool", "content": '{"results": [{"file": "login.py", ...}, ...]}'},
242:]
243:
244:result = engine.compress_messages(messages)
245:
246:# Cross-message dedup runs first, then per-message pipeline
247:print(result["stats"]["reduction_pct"])   # aggregate compression %
248:print(result["per_message"])              # per-message breakdown
249:```
250:
251:### Rewind — Reversible Retrieval
252:
253:```python
254:engine = FusionEngine(enable_rewind=True)
255:result = engine.compress(large_json, content_type="json")
256:
257:# LLM sees compressed output with markers like [rewind:abc123...]
258:# When the LLM needs the original, it calls the Rewind tool:
259:original = engine.rewind_store.retrieve("abc123def456...")
260:```
261:
262:### Custom Stage
263:
264:```python
265:from scripts.lib.fusion.base import FusionStage, FusionContext, FusionResult
266:
267:class MyStage(FusionStage):
268:    name = "my_compressor"
269:    order = 22  # runs between StructuralCollapse (20) and Neurosyntax (25)
270:
271:    def should_apply(self, ctx: FusionContext) -> bool:
272:        return ctx.content_type == "log"
273:
274:    def apply(self, ctx: FusionContext) -> FusionResult:
275:        compressed = my_compression_logic(ctx.content)
276:        return FusionResult(
277:            content=compressed,
278:            original_tokens=estimate_tokens(ctx.content),
279:            compressed_tokens=estimate_tokens(compressed),
280:        )
281:
282:# Add to pipeline
283:pipeline = engine.pipeline.add(MyStage())
284:```
285:
286:---
287:
288:## The 14 Stages
289:
290:| # | Stage | Order | Purpose | Applies To |
291:|:-:|:------|:-----:|:--------|:-----------|
292:| 1 | **QuantumLock** | 3 | Isolates dynamic content in system prompts to maximize KV-cache hit rate | system messages |
293:| 2 | **Cortex** | 5 | Auto-detects content type and programming language (16 languages) | untyped content |
294:| 3 | **Photon** | 8 | Detects and compresses base64-encoded images | all |
295:| 4 | **RLE** | 10 | Path shorthand (`$WS`), IP prefix compression, enum compaction | all |
296:| 5 | **SemanticDedup** | 12 | SimHash fingerprint deduplication across content blocks | all |
297:| 6 | **Ionizer** | 15 | JSON array statistical sampling with schema discovery + error preservation | json |
298:| 7 | **LogCrunch** | 16 | Folds repeated log lines with occurrence counts | log |
299:| 8 | **SearchCrunch** | 17 | Deduplicates search/grep results | search |
300:| 9 | **DiffCrunch** | 18 | Folds unchanged context lines in git diffs | diff |
301:| 10 | **StructuralCollapse** | 20 | Merges import blocks, collapses repeated assertions/patterns | code |
302:| 11 | **Neurosyntax** | 25 | AST-aware code compression via tree-sitter (safe regex fallback). Never shortens identifiers. | code |
303:| 12 | **Nexus** | 35 | ML token-level compression (stopword removal fallback without model) | text |
304:| 13 | **TokenOpt** | 40 | Tokenizer format optimization — strips bold/italic markers, normalizes whitespace | all |
305:| 14 | **Abbrev** | 45 | Natural language abbreviation. Only fires on text — never touches code, JSON, or structured data. | text |
306:
307:Each stage is independent and stateless. Stages communicate only through the immutable `FusionContext` that flows forward through the pipeline.
308:
309:---
310:
311:## Workspace Commands
312:
313:```bash
314:python3 scripts/mem_compress.py <workspace> <command> [options]
315:```
316:
317:| Command | Description |
318:|:--------|:-----------|
319:| `full` | Run complete compression pipeline |
320:| `benchmark` | Dry-run compression report |
321:| `compress` | Rule-based compression only |
322:| `dict` | Dictionary encoding with auto-learned codebook |
323:| `observe` | Session transcript JSONL to structured observations |
324:| `tiers` | Generate L0/L1/L2 tiered summaries |
325:| `dedup` | Cross-file duplicate detection |
326:| `estimate` | Token count report |
327:| `audit` | Workspace health check |
328:| `optimize` | Tokenizer-level format optimization |
329:| `auto` | Watch mode — compress on file changes |
330:
331:Options: `--json`, `--dry-run`, `--since YYYY-MM-DD`, `--quiet`

1:# claude-code-cache-fix
2:
3:[![npm](https://img.shields.io/npm/v/claude-code-cache-fix?color=blue)](https://www.npmjs.com/package/claude-code-cache-fix) [![Node.js](https://img.shields.io/badge/Node.js-18%2B-green)](https://nodejs.org/) [![License: MIT](https://img.shields.io/badge/License-MIT-yellow)](https://opensource.org/licenses/MIT) [![GitHub stars](https://img.shields.io/github/stars/cnighswonger/claude-code-cache-fix)](https://github.com/cnighswonger/claude-code-cache-fix/stargazers)
4:
5:English | [中文](./README.zh.md) | [한국어](./README.ko.md) | [Português](./docs/guia-pt-br.md)
6:
7:Cache optimization proxy for [Claude Code](https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code). Fixes prompt cache bugs that cause excessive quota burn, stabilizes the request prefix, and monitors for silent regressions. Works with all CC versions including the v2.1.113+ Bun binary.
8:
9:> **v3.0.3** — Local HTTP proxy with 7 hot-reloadable extensions. A/B tested on v2.1.117: **95.5% cache hit rate through proxy vs 82.3% direct** on first warm turn. [Full release notes →](https://github.com/cnighswonger/claude-code-cache-fix/releases/tag/v3.0.0)
10:
11:> **Opus 4.7 advisory:** Metered data shows 4.7 burns Q5h quota at **~2.4x the rate of 4.6** for equivalent visible token counts ([independently confirmed by @ArkNill](https://github.com/ArkNill/claude-code-hidden-problem-analysis/blob/main/16_OPUS-47-ADVISORY.md)). Two factors: a new tokenizer (up to 35% more tokens, [documented](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/models/whats-new-claude-4-7)) and adaptive thinking overhead (~105%, not documented in usage response). The Q5h impact compounds into **Q7d** — the weekly quota ceiling that most heavy users will hit first. Workaround: `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_ADAPTIVE_THINKING=1` reduces burn by ~3.3x but may reduce quality on complex tasks. See [Discussion #25](https://github.com/cnighswonger/claude-code-cache-fix/discussions/25) (initial observation) and [Discussion #42](https://github.com/cnighswonger/claude-code-cache-fix/discussions/42) (controlled A/B data + Q7d analysis).
12:
13:## Quick Start: Proxy (recommended)
14:
15:The proxy works with any CC version — Node.js or Bun binary. It sits between Claude Code and the Anthropic API, applying cache fixes as hot-reloadable extensions.
16:
17:```bash
18:# Install
19:npm install -g claude-code-cache-fix
20:
21:# Start the proxy (runs on localhost:9801)
22:node "$(npm root -g)/claude-code-cache-fix/proxy/server.mjs" &
23:
24:# Launch Claude Code through it
25:ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:9801 claude
26:```
27:
28:That's it. The proxy applies all 7 cache-fix extensions automatically. No wrapper scripts, no `NODE_OPTIONS`, no preload.
29:
30:### What the proxy does
31:
32:On every `/v1/messages` request, 7 extensions run in order:
33:
34:| Extension | What it fixes |
35:|-----------|--------------|
36:| `fingerprint-strip` | Removes unstable cc_version fingerprint from system prompt |
37:| `sort-stabilization` | Deterministic ordering of tool and MCP definitions |
38:| `ttl-management` | Detects server TTL tier, injects correct cache_control markers |
39:| `identity-normalization` | Normalizes message identity fields for prefix stability |
40:| `fresh-session-sort` | Fixes non-deterministic ordering on first turn |
41:| `cache-control-normalize` | Normalizes cache_control markers across messages |
42:| `cache-telemetry` | Extracts cache stats from response headers → `~/.claude/quota-status.json` |
43:
44:Extensions are hot-reloadable — add, remove, or modify `.mjs` files in `proxy/extensions/` and changes apply to the next request without restarting. Configuration in `proxy/extensions.json`.
45:
46:### Running as a service
47:
48:**Recommended (Linux/macOS) — `install-service` subcommand:**
49:
50:```bash
51:cache-fix-proxy install-service
52:```
53:
54:Detects your platform and writes the appropriate config:
55:
56:- **Linux** → `~/.config/systemd/user/cache-fix-proxy.service` (systemd user unit)
57:- **macOS** → `~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.cnighswonger.cache-fix-proxy.plist` (launchd agent)
58:
59:The output prints the next-step commands to enable and start the service. On Linux:
60:
61:```bash
62:systemctl --user daemon-reload
63:systemctl --user enable --now cache-fix-proxy
64:systemctl --user enable --now cache-fix-proxy-healthcheck.timer   # auto-recovery — see below
65:sudo loginctl enable-linger $USER   # optional: start on boot, not just on login
66:```
67:
68:**Auto-recovery (Linux):** `install-service` also drops a healthcheck companion (`cache-fix-proxy-healthcheck.service` + `.timer`). The timer fires every 2 minutes; the oneshot service runs `curl -fs http://127.0.0.1:<port>/health` and `systemctl --user start cache-fix-proxy.service` if the probe fails. This recovers the proxy from any stop — clean or unclean, expected or unexpected — within 2 minutes. Background: `Restart=on-failure` doesn't fire on clean stops, so before this companion existed, a `systemctl stop` from any source (including unidentified ones during an Anthropic outage on 2026-04-25) would leave the proxy down indefinitely. macOS doesn't need the companion — launchd's `KeepAlive` already auto-restarts on any exit.
69:
70:On macOS:
71:
72:```bash
73:launchctl bootstrap gui/$(id -u) ~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.cnighswonger.cache-fix-proxy.plist
74:launchctl enable gui/$(id -u)/com.cnighswonger.cache-fix-proxy
75:launchctl kickstart gui/$(id -u)/com.cnighswonger.cache-fix-proxy
76:```
77:
78:The installed config picks up `CACHE_FIX_PROXY_PORT`, `CACHE_FIX_PROXY_UPSTREAM`, and `CACHE_FIX_DEBUG` from the env at install time. Re-run `install-service --force` to regenerate after env changes, or edit the service file directly. Pair with `cache-fix-proxy uninstall-service` to remove cleanly (stops, disables, deletes).
79:
80:The service runs `cache-fix-proxy server` in the foreground, which is just the proxy without the wrapper-mode claude launcher.
81:
82:**Manual (any platform):**
83:
84:```bash
85:nohup cache-fix-proxy server > /tmp/cache-fix-proxy.log 2>&1 &
86:echo 'export ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:9801' >> ~/.bashrc
87:```
88:
89:### Docker
90:
91:A multi-arch (amd64, arm64) container image is published to GitHub Container Registry on every release tag.
92:
93:```bash
94:docker run -d --name cache-fix-proxy \
95:  --restart=always \
96:  -p 9801:9801 \
97:  ghcr.io/cnighswonger/claude-code-cache-fix:latest
98:
99:# Then in your shell:
100:export ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:9801
101:```
102:
103:Use `--restart=always` instead of the systemd healthcheck companion — Docker handles auto-recovery natively. Mount nothing; the container is stateless. Override the default port with `-e CACHE_FIX_PROXY_PORT=...`. Override the upstream (e.g. to chain through llm-relay) with `-e CACHE_FIX_PROXY_UPSTREAM=http://host.docker.internal:8080`. The image runs as the unprivileged `node` user (uid 1000) and exposes a `HEALTHCHECK` Docker can use for liveness.
104:
105:For corporate environments behind an SSL-inspecting proxy, mount your CA bundle and set the env vars:
106:
107:```bash
108:docker run -d --name cache-fix-proxy --restart=always -p 9801:9801 \
109:  -e HTTPS_PROXY=http://proxy.corp.example:8080 \
110:  -e CACHE_FIX_PROXY_CA_FILE=/etc/ssl/corp-ca.pem \
111:  -v /path/to/zscaler-root.pem:/etc/ssl/corp-ca.pem:ro \
112:  ghcr.io/cnighswonger/claude-code-cache-fix:latest
113:```
114:
115:Image tags: `latest`, `3`, `3.2`, `3.2.1` (semver-ladder, so `3` always points to the newest 3.x). `latest` always tracks the newest tagged release.
116:
117:**Linux note:** the chained-upstream `host.docker.internal` example below is automatic on Docker Desktop (macOS / Windows). On plain Linux Docker Engine you usually need `--add-host=host.docker.internal:host-gateway` so the name resolves to the host bridge. Without it, the container's name lookup fails and the proxy can't reach the upstream service running on the host. Example chaining cache-fix proxy through `llm-relay` running on the host:
118:
119:```bash
120:docker run -d --name cache-fix-proxy --restart=always -p 9801:9801 \
121:  --add-host=host.docker.internal:host-gateway \
122:  -e CACHE_FIX_PROXY_UPSTREAM=http://host.docker.internal:8080 \
123:  ghcr.io/cnighswonger/claude-code-cache-fix:latest
124:```
125:
126:### Health check
127:
128:```bash
129:curl http://127.0.0.1:9801/health
130:# {"status":"ok"}
131:```
132:
133:### Proxy configuration
134:
135:All proxy settings are controlled via environment variables. Set them before starting the proxy server.
136:
137:| Variable | Default | Description |
138:|----------|---------|-------------|
139:| `CACHE_FIX_PROXY_PORT` | `9801` | Listen port |
140:| `CACHE_FIX_PROXY_BIND` | `127.0.0.1` | Bind address |
141:| `CACHE_FIX_PROXY_UPSTREAM` | `https://api.anthropic.com` | Upstream URL. Change to chain another proxy (e.g. `http://localhost:8080`) |
142:| `CACHE_FIX_PROXY_TIMEOUT` | `600000` | Request timeout in milliseconds |
143:| `CACHE_FIX_EXTENSIONS_DIR` | `proxy/extensions/` | Directory for extension `.mjs` files |
144:| `CACHE_FIX_EXTENSIONS_CONFIG` | `proxy/extensions.json` | Extension configuration file |
145:| `CACHE_FIX_DEBUG` | `0` | Enable debug logging |
146:
147:### Corporate environments (proxies, custom CAs)
148:
149:The proxy honors the following environment variables when forwarding to `api.anthropic.com`. Behind Zscaler / Netskope / Forcepoint / Bluecoat / corporate squid, set these in the proxy's environment.
150:
151:| Variable | Effect |
152:|----------|--------|
153:| `HTTPS_PROXY` / `HTTP_PROXY` (and lowercase variants) | Routes upstream requests through the corporate HTTP CONNECT proxy. |
154:| `NO_PROXY` | Comma-separated host list to bypass the proxy. Supports `*` and `.suffix.example.com`. |
155:| `CACHE_FIX_PROXY_CA_FILE` | Path to a PEM file with one or more extra CA certificates (for SSL-inspecting proxies). |
156:| `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS` | Standard Node mechanism — also honored. |
157:| `CACHE_FIX_PROXY_REJECT_UNAUTHORIZED=0` | **Insecure escape hatch.** Disables TLS verification. Use only as a last resort while you wait for IT to provide the corp CA bundle. |
158:
159:Example (Windows PowerShell):
160:
161:```powershell
162:$env:HTTPS_PROXY = 'http://proxy.corp.example:8080'
163:$env:NO_PROXY    = 'localhost,127.0.0.1,.corp.example'
164:$env:CACHE_FIX_PROXY_CA_FILE = 'C:\corp\zscaler-root.pem'
165:node "$(npm root -g)\claude-code-cache-fix\proxy\server.mjs"
166:```
167:
168:Stderr will print `[upstream] using proxy http://proxy.corp.example:8080 ...` on first request when the agent is wired correctly. With no proxy/CA env vars set, behavior is unchanged from earlier versions (Node default agent, system trust store).
169:

171:
172:If you're on a Node.js-based CC version (v2.1.112 or earlier), the preload interceptor works without a proxy:
173:
174:```bash
175:npm install -g claude-code-cache-fix
176:NODE_OPTIONS="--import claude-code-cache-fix" claude
177:```
178:
179:> **Note:** The preload does NOT work on CC v2.1.113+ (Bun binary). Use the proxy above.

184:

186:
187:**Proxy mode (recommended):**
188:1. Start the proxy (see above)
189:2. In VS Code command palette: **Claude Code Cache Fix: Enable Proxy Mode**
190:3. Restart any active Claude Code session

194:2. Download the VSIX from [GitHub Releases](https://github.com/cnighswonger/claude-code-cache-fix-vscode/releases/latest)
195:3. Install: `code --install-extension claude-code-cache-fix-0.5.0.vsix`
196:4. Command palette: **Claude Code Cache Fix: Enable**

199:
200:## Security model
201:
202:> **The proxy and interceptor have full read/write access to API requests and responses.** This is inherent to the approach — any fetch interceptor, proxy, or gateway has this position.
203:
204:**What it does:** Modifies outgoing request structure (block order, fingerprint, TTL, git-status) to fix cache bugs. Reads response headers and SSE usage data for monitoring.
205:
206:**What it does NOT do:** No network calls from the proxy or interceptor. All telemetry is written to local files under `~/.claude/`. No data leaves your machine.
207:
208:**Supply chain:** Proxy mode: 7 small extension modules in `proxy/extensions/` (each under 200 lines). Preload mode: single unminified file (`preload.mjs`, ~1,700 lines). One dev dependency (`zod` for schema validation in tests only). Review before installing. npm provenance links each published version to its source commit.
209:
210:**Independent audit:** [Assessed as "LEGITIMATE TOOL"](https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code/issues/38335#issuecomment-4244413605) by @TheAuditorTool (2026-04-14).
211:
212:## The problem
213:
214:When you use `--resume` or `/resume` in Claude Code, the prompt cache breaks silently. Instead of reading cached tokens (cheap), the API rebuilds them from scratch on every turn (expensive). A session that should cost ~$0.50/hour can burn through $5–10/hour with no visible indication anything is wrong.
215:
216:Three bugs cause this:
217:
218:1. **Partial block scatter** — Attachment blocks (skills listing, MCP servers, deferred tools, hooks) are supposed to live in `messages[0]`. On resume, some or all drift to later messages, changing the cache prefix.
219:
220:2. **Fingerprint instability** — The `cc_version` fingerprint (e.g. `2.1.92.a3f`) is computed from `messages[0]` content including meta/attachment blocks. When those blocks shift, the fingerprint changes, the system prompt changes, and cache busts.
221:
222:3. **Non-deterministic tool ordering** — Tool definitions can arrive in different orders between turns, changing request bytes and invalidating the cache key.
223:
224:Additionally, images read via the Read tool persist as base64 in conversation history and are sent on every subsequent API call, compounding token costs silently.
225:
226:## How it works
227:
228:**Proxy mode** (v3.0.0+): An HTTP server on `localhost:9801` intercepts `POST /v1/messages` requests. Seven extension modules process each request through a pipeline — normalizing block order, stripping fingerprints, stabilizing tool sort, managing TTL markers. Extensions are hot-reloadable `.mjs` files configured in `proxy/extensions.json`. All other traffic passes through untouched.
229:
230:**Preload mode** (v2.x): A Node.js `--import` module that patches `globalThis.fetch` before Claude Code makes API calls. Applies the same fixes inline — scans user messages for relocated blocks, sorts tools, recomputes fingerprints, injects TTL markers.
231:
232:Both modes are idempotent — if nothing needs fixing, the request passes through unmodified. Neither mode modifies your conversation; they only normalize the request structure before it hits the API.
233:
234:## Graduating from fixes
235:
236:The package serves three purposes with different lifecycles:
237:
238:| Purpose | Examples | When to disable |
239:|---------|----------|-----------------|
240:| **Bug fixes** | Block relocation, fingerprint, tool sort, TTL | When CC fixes the underlying bug — check the health line |
241:| **Monitoring** | Quota tracking, microcompact detection, GrowthBook flags | Keep permanently — these detect future regressions |
242:| **Optimizations** | Image stripping, output efficiency rewrite | Keep as long as they help your workflow |
243:
244:### Health status (preload mode)
245:
246:On first API call, the interceptor logs a health status line (requires `CACHE_FIX_DEBUG=1`):
247:
248:```
249:cache-fix health: relocate=active(2h ago) fingerprint=dormant(5 clean sessions) tool_sort=active ttl=active identity=waiting
250:```
251:
252:- **active(Xh ago)** — fix was applied recently
253:- **dormant(N clean sessions)** — bug not detected in N sessions; CC may have fixed it
254:- **safety-blocked(Nx)** — round-trip verification failed; fix auto-disabled
255:- **waiting** — fix hasn't been triggered yet
256:
257:### Regression detection
258:
259:If cache_read ratio drops below 50% across 5+ calls after disabling fixes:
260:```
261:REGRESSION WARNING: cache_read ratio averaged 12% across last 5 calls.
262:Fixes are disabled — consider re-enabling to recover cache performance.
263:```
264:
265:## Safety
266:
267:### Fingerprint round-trip verification
268:
269:Before rewriting the `cc_version` fingerprint, the interceptor verifies that its hardcoded salt and character indices reproduce the fingerprint Claude Code sent. If verification fails (CC changed its algorithm), the rewrite is skipped automatically. This ensures the interceptor can never make cache performance *worse* than stock CC.
270:
271:### Fail-safe design
272:
273:Every fix is designed to fail to a no-op:
274:- If block detection regexes don't match → blocks aren't relocated (CC behavior)
275:- If fingerprint format changes → fingerprint isn't rewritten (CC behavior)
276:- If tool sort produces no changes → payload passes through untouched
277:- If TTL injection target structure changes → TTL isn't injected (CC behavior)
278:
279:The interceptor can only *help* or *do nothing*. It cannot make things worse.
280:
281:## Status line — quota warnings in real time
282:
283:Both proxy and preload modes write quota state to `~/.claude/quota-status.json` on every API call. The included `tools/quota-statusline.sh` script displays a live status line showing:
284:
285:- **Q5h %** with burn rate (%/min)
286:- **Q7d %** with burn rate (%/hr)
287:- **TTL tier** — `TTL:1h` when healthy, **`TTL:5m` in red when the server has downgraded you** (typically at Q5h ≥ 100%)
288:- **PEAK** in yellow during weekday peak hours (13:00–19:00 UTC)
289:- **Cache hit rate %**
290:- **OVERAGE** flag when active
291:
292:### Setup
293:
294:```bash
295:mkdir -p ~/.claude/hooks
296:cp "$(npm root -g)/claude-code-cache-fix/tools/quota-statusline.sh" ~/.claude/hooks/
297:chmod +x ~/.claude/hooks/quota-statusline.sh
298:```
299:
300:Add to `~/.claude/settings.json`:
301:
302:```json
303:{
304:  "statusLine": {
305:    "type": "command",
306:    "command": "~/.claude/hooks/quota-statusline.sh"
307:  }
308:}
309:```
310:
311:### Why the status line matters
312:
313:When the server downgrades your TTL to 5m (quota-aware downgrade at Q5h ≥ 100%), **every idle longer than 5 minutes causes a full context rebuild**. Without the status line, this is invisible. With it, the red `TTL:5m` warning tells you: **stop working, wait for the Q5h window to reset, then resume**. Powering through overage compounds the drain; pausing breaks the cycle.
314:
315:### Recommended: disable git-status injection
316:
317:Claude Code injects live `git status` into the system prompt on every call. Any file edit changes the git status, which busts the entire prefix cache. Disabling this saves ~1,800 tokens per call:
318:
319:```bash
320:export CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_GIT_INSTRUCTIONS=1
321:```
322:
323:Or add `"includeGitInstructions": false` to `~/.claude/settings.json`. Claude Code can still run `git status` via the Bash tool when it needs context. Community-validated by [@wadabum](https://github.com/cnighswonger/claude-code-cache-fix/issues/11): 18-token cache creation across git state changes (vs thousands without the flag).
324:
325:**Why we don't ship a proxy extension for this:** the proxy intercepts requests after Claude Code has already composed the system prompt — by then the volatile `git status` text is already part of the prefix that the model conditioned on in the previous turn, and stripping it post-hoc would itself bust the cache. The fix has to happen at the source. `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_GIT_INSTRUCTIONS=1` prevents the injection before the prompt is composed, which is why the native flag is the right tool. Stripping post-hoc would also remove model-visible context that an explicit Bash call can recover, and would risk false-positive matches against assistant-written text.
326:
327:## Image stripping (preload mode)
328:
329:Images read via the Read tool persist as base64 in conversation history, riding along on every subsequent API call. A single 500KB image costs ~62,500 tokens per turn on Opus 4.6, and **~85,000+ on Opus 4.7** due to the new tokenizer. Image stripping is strongly recommended on 4.7.
330:
331:```bash
332:export CACHE_FIX_IMAGE_KEEP_LAST=3
333:```
334:
335:Keeps images in the last 3 user messages, replaces older ones with a text placeholder. Only targets `tool_result` blocks — user-pasted images are never touched.
336:
337:### Oversized-image guard (legacy, v3.2.1)
338:
339:```bash
340:export CACHE_FIX_IMAGE_MAX_DIM=2000
341:```
342:
343:The Anthropic API enforces TWO image-related limits on multi-image requests, and the same error message can fire for either:
344:
345:> `"An image in the conversation exceeds the dimension limit for many-image requests (2000px). Start a new session with fewer images."`
346:
347:Two pressure axes to address them:
348:
349:| Pressure | Variable | What it does |
350:|---|---|---|
351:| **Too many images in conversation** | `CACHE_FIX_IMAGE_KEEP_LAST=N` | Strips images from old user messages, keeps only the last N. |
352:| **Any single image too large** | `CACHE_FIX_IMAGE_MAX_DIM=2000` | Replaces images exceeding the dimension limit with a forensic placeholder noting the original dimensions. Covers both user-message direct images and tool_result-nested images. |
353:
354:The two compose: with both set, `KEEP_LAST` runs first (drops the count), then `MAX_DIM` runs on what remains (caps the size of the kept ones). Common triggers for the dimension axis: hi-res manuscript scans, retina screenshots, photos at full resolution.
355:
356:Pure-JS PNG and JPEG header parsing — no native deps. Other formats (GIF, WebP, AVIF, BMP) pass through unchanged regardless of dimension. Fail-open: images whose dimensions can't be parsed (truncated header, unsupported format) are kept rather than stripped — better to send a request that might error than to strip a valid image we just couldn't measure.
357:
358:### Image-guard pipeline (v3.3.0)
359:
360:A conditional pipeline that mirrors Anthropic's actual rules. Strictly opt-in via a single env var:
361:
362:```bash
363:export CACHE_FIX_IMAGE_GUARD=1
364:```
365:
366:When enabled, the proxy runs:
367:
368:| Pass | Trigger | Action |
369:|------|---------|--------|
370:| **Pass 0** (legacy) | `CACHE_FIX_IMAGE_KEEP_LAST=N` set | Strip tool_result images from user messages older than N most recent |
371:| **Pass 3** | `CACHE_FIX_IMAGE_PRESERVE_DETAIL=1` AND image long edge > model native cap | Lanczos resize via `sharp` to native cap (2576 px for Opus 4.7, 1568 px otherwise), preserve aspect ratio and media type |
372:| **Pass 1** | image long edge > active rejection cap | Strip and replace with forensic placeholder. Active cap = `MAX_DIM` if set, else 2000 px (when count > 20) or 8000 px (count ≤ 20) |
373:| **Pass 2** | request body exceeds `CACHE_FIX_IMAGE_REQUEST_SIZE_MAX` (default 30 MB) | Drop oldest images until under budget |
374:| **Count cap** | surviving image count > `CACHE_FIX_IMAGE_COUNT_MAX` (default 100) | Drop oldest images down to the cap |
375:
376:Execution order: **Pass 0 → Pass 3 → Pass 1 → Pass 2 → count cap**. Each pass is independent — Pass 1 never resizes; Pass 3 never strips.
377:
378:#### Optional `sharp` dependency
379:
380:Pass 3 requires [sharp](https://www.npmjs.com/package/sharp) for Lanczos resize. It's declared as an **optional peer dependency** — install separately if you want Pass 3:
381:
382:```bash
383:npm install sharp
384:```
385:
386:If `sharp` is missing, Pass 3 skips cleanly (telemetry records `library_missing: true`); Pass 1 + Pass 2 + the count cap still run.
387:
388:#### Precedence matrix
389:
390:| Env var combination | Behavior |
391:|---|---|
392:| Nothing set | No image processing (back-compat default; the extension short-circuits). |
393:| `KEEP_LAST=N` only | Existing v3.2.1: count cap on tool_result images in user messages, runs first. No pipeline. |
394:| `MAX_DIM=N` only | Existing v3.2.1: hard size cap, strip-only. No pipeline. |
395:| `KEEP_LAST=N` + `MAX_DIM=N` | Existing v3.2.1 composition: `KEEP_LAST` runs first (drops count), then `MAX_DIM` runs on survivors (caps size). No pipeline, no Pass 2, no Pass 3. |
396:| `IMAGE_GUARD=1` | New pipeline: Pass 1 (conditional cap) + Pass 2 (request-size guard) + image-count cap. |
397:| `IMAGE_GUARD=1` + `MAX_DIM=N` | `MAX_DIM` overrides Pass 1's conditional cap (acts as the cap value); Pass 2 still runs. |
398:| `IMAGE_GUARD=1` + `PRESERVE_DETAIL=1` | Adds Pass 3 (Lanczos resize via `sharp`). When `sharp` unavailable, falls back to strip behavior. |
399:| `IMAGE_GUARD=1` + `KEEP_LAST=N` | `KEEP_LAST` runs first as count cap (Pass 0); pipeline runs on remainder. |
400:| `IMAGE_GUARD=1` + `KEEP_LAST=N` + `MAX_DIM=N` | Three-way: `KEEP_LAST` runs first; pipeline runs on remainder, but `MAX_DIM` overrides Pass 1's conditional cap; Pass 2 still runs. |
401:| `PRESERVE_DETAIL=1` without `IMAGE_GUARD=1` | Logs warning, treats as no-op. `PRESERVE_DETAIL` is meaningless without the pipeline running. |
402:
403:#### Tunables
404:
405:| Env var | Default | Purpose |
406:|---------|---------|---------|
407:| `CACHE_FIX_IMAGE_GUARD` | unset | Top-level pipeline gate (`=1` enables). |
408:| `CACHE_FIX_IMAGE_PRESERVE_DETAIL` | unset | Enable Pass 3 Lanczos resize via `sharp`. |
409:| `CACHE_FIX_IMAGE_REQUEST_SIZE_MAX` | 31457280 (30 MB) | Pass 2 byte budget. 2 MB headroom from Anthropic's 32 MB ceiling. |
410:| `CACHE_FIX_IMAGE_COUNT_MAX` | 100 | Hard image-count cap. Set to 600 for legacy Claude 1/2.x/Instant if needed. |
411:
412:## Cache breakpoints (proxy mode, opt-in)
413:
414:Anthropic's prompt cache supports up to **four** `cache_control` markers per request. Claude Code currently uses three of the four; the third (between auto-injected `messages[0]` content — hooks, skills, project CLAUDE.md, deferred tools, MCP server descriptions — and the first real user content) is missing entirely. Without that marker, every change inside the auto-injected span busts the cache for everything that follows. wadabum projected ~6,500 token savings per fresh-session first turn from adding it ([anthropics/claude-code#47098](https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code/issues/47098)).
415:
416:The proxy can inject the missing marker on opt-in. Default off until validated against community data.
417:
418:```sh
419:export CACHE_FIX_INJECT_MESSAGES_BREAKPOINT=1
420:```
421:
422:The injection is conservative: it only fires when the request already carries 1–3 markers (typical CC shape) and refuses if the request is at the 4-marker limit (would 400) or has zero markers (Agent SDK / API-direct shape this extension isn't built for). Boundary detection covers all five observed auto-injected block kinds — hooks, skills, CLAUDE.md, deferred-tools, MCP — and lands the marker on the LAST auto-injected block.
423:
424:A diagnostic-only env var dumps the structural shape of `messages[0]` for fixture sourcing without mutating the request:
425:
426:```sh
427:export CACHE_FIX_DUMP_MESSAGES_HEAD=/tmp/messages-head.jsonl
428:```
429:
430:| Env var | Default | Purpose |
431:|---------|---------|---------|
432:| `CACHE_FIX_INJECT_MESSAGES_BREAKPOINT` | unset | Enable breakpoint #3 injection (`=1` opt-in). |
433:| `CACHE_FIX_DUMP_MESSAGES_HEAD` | unset | Diagnostic JSONL dump of `messages[0].content` shape — read-only, no mutation. |
434:
435:## Microcompact stability (proxy mode, opt-in)
436:
437:After ~90 minutes idle, Claude Code's `time_based_microcompact` (and the cold-compact path triggered by `FDY()`) replaces old `tool_result` content with a sentinel string. The original content is gone for cache purposes; that part is unrecoverable from the proxy. But the sentinel itself can carry an embedded timestamp (`[Old tool result content cleared at 2026-04-30T13:42:11Z]`), which means a *second* microcompact pass against the same already-cleared position writes different bytes — busting the cache for everything after that position even though no new content was added.
438:
439:This extension addresses the recoverable half: normalize the sentinel to a byte-stable canonical form so repeat microcompacts don't churn the cache. **Phase 1 only** — diagnostic + opt-in normalization. Phase 2 (snapshot-and-restore of original tool_result content) is deferred to v3.5.0+ pending Phase 1 production data.
440:
441:```sh
442:# Step 1 (diagnostic): characterize what CC's sentinel actually looks like.
443:export CACHE_FIX_DUMP_MICROCOMPACT=/tmp/microcompact-dump.jsonl
444:
445:# Step 2 (normalize): once the sentinel format is confirmed, opt-in.
446:export CACHE_FIX_NORMALIZE_MICROCOMPACT=1
447:```
448:
449:Detection has two modes:
450:- **Mode A** — exact match against confirmed CC sentinel patterns (the bare form and the ISO-8601 timestamp variant). Mode A matches are eligible for normalization.
451:- **Mode B** — prefix-only match (text begins with `[Old tool result content cleared` but does not exactly match a Mode A pattern). Mode B is **diagnostic-only**: never normalized, dump records redact to a 64-char prefix only.
452:
453:The Mode A/B separation protects against cases where the sentinel might be followed by user-derived content (e.g., a tool that echoed user input back into its result) — the redaction guarantee on Mode B keeps that content out of the diagnostic dump.
454:
455:| Env var | Default | Purpose |
456:|---------|---------|---------|
457:| `CACHE_FIX_DUMP_MICROCOMPACT` | unset | Path for diagnostic JSONL dump of detected sentinels. Read-only — no mutation. |
458:| `CACHE_FIX_NORMALIZE_MICROCOMPACT` | unset | Enable normalization (`=1` opts in). Mutates Mode A matches to canonical form. |
459:| `CACHE_FIX_MICROCOMPACT_NORMALIZED` | `[Old tool result content cleared]` | Override the canonical replacement string. |
460:| `CACHE_FIX_MICROCOMPACT_SENTINEL_PATTERN_<N>` | unset | Add custom Mode A regex pattern(s). Numbered (1-indexed, sparse OK). |
461:| `CACHE_FIX_MICROCOMPACT_SENTINEL_PREFIX_<N>` | unset | Custom Mode B literal prefix(es). Pair with a custom Mode A pattern from a non-default sentinel family so prefix-only variants of that family also get redacted Mode B capture. |
462:| `CACHE_FIX_MICROCOMPACT_REDACT_LEN` | `64` | Mode B prefix length in dump records. Set to `0` to suppress the prefix entirely. |
463:| `CACHE_FIX_DUMP_MICROCOMPACT_INCLUDE_NORMALIZED` | unset | Add post-normalization text alongside (not replacing) raw `sentinel_text` in dump records. |
464:
465:## System prompt rewrite (preload mode, optional)
466:
467:The interceptor can rewrite Claude Code's `# Output efficiency` system-prompt section. Disabled by default. Enable with `CACHE_FIX_OUTPUT_EFFICIENCY_REPLACEMENT`. See [docs/output-efficiency-prompts.md](docs/output-efficiency-prompts.md) for the three known prompt variants and usage instructions.
468:
469:## Monitoring & diagnostics
470:
471:The preload interceptor includes monitoring for microcompact degradation, false rate limiters, GrowthBook flag state, usage telemetry, and cost reporting. Quota tracking works in both proxy and preload modes via `~/.claude/quota-status.json`.
472:
473:See [docs/monitoring.md](docs/monitoring.md) for full details, debug mode, prefix diffing, environment variables, and the bundled quota analysis tool.
474:
475:## Limitations
476:
477:- **Proxy requires a running process** — The proxy must be started before Claude Code. If it's not running and `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` points to it, CC will fail to connect. We recommend running it as a systemd service or with a health-checking wrapper script.
478:- **Overage TTL downgrade** — Exceeding 100% of the 5-hour quota triggers a server-enforced TTL downgrade from 1h to 5m. This is server-side and cannot be fixed client-side. The proxy/interceptor prevents the cache instability that can push you into overage in the first place.
479:- **Microcompact is not preventable** — The monitoring features detect context degradation but cannot prevent it. Microcompact and budget enforcement are server-controlled via GrowthBook flags with no client-side disable option.
480:- **System prompt rewrite is experimental** — Preload-only, opt-in. Not proven to be the cause of behavior differences discussed in community reports. Use at your own risk.
481:- **Version coupling** — The fingerprint salt and block detection heuristics are derived from Claude Code internals. A major refactor could require an update to this package.
482:

488:
489:- **[@ArkNill/claude-code-hidden-problem-analysis](https://github.com/ArkNill/claude-code-hidden-problem-analysis)** — 38,996-request proxy-based analysis: 7 bugs (microcompact, budget caps, false rate limiter, JSONL duplication, extended thinking), GrowthBook feature flag causal testing, Opus 4.7 burn rate advisory. The monitoring features in v1.1.0 are informed by this research.
490:- **[@Renvect/X-Ray-Claude-Code-Interceptor](https://github.com/Renvect/X-Ray-Claude-Code-Interceptor)** — Diagnostic HTTPS proxy with real-time dashboard, system prompt section diffing, per-tool stripping thresholds. Works with any Claude client that supports `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`.
491:- **[@fgrosswig/claude-usage-dashboard](https://github.com/fgrosswig/claude-usage-dashboard)** — Self-hosted forensic dashboard with SSE live monitoring, multi-host aggregation, cache-health scoring. Complementary to our proxy's vantage point. See [docs/dashboard-integration.md](docs/dashboard-integration.md) for the interop setup.

Identified two distinct cache regression patterns through real-world testing — tool ordering jitter and the fresh-session sort gap — and contributed debug traces that drove the v1.5.1 and v1.6.2 fixes.
